{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CBrWDWsrrDWuE4taNsb9Di","type":"tool_result","content":"# recs_eval release notes\n\n## 1.4.2 (2026-08-20)\n- `ope` writes the four files in `docs/outputs/ope_outputs.md` so the launch review has one artefact.\n\n## 1.4.0 (2026-07-02)\n- Offline gate: replay a candidate ranker on logged responses and report clicks per matched impression, scaled to a\n  five-slot row (`CTR@5`).\n- Gate runs on production-stream responses, where the candidate lists are logged.\n\n## 1.3.0 (2026-05-11)\n- Candidate list logging (`rec_candidates`) sampled for production traffic; all exploration requests are logged.\n# Recs / Experimentation sync, 2 September 2026\n\n**Ranking (v7 owner).** The gate had v7 well ahead of v6 before we ran AB-1182, and the gate has been our launch\ncriterion all year. The A/B came back negative with a sample-ratio warning, so the experiment is the thing to\ndistrust, not the model. We would like to ship v7.\n\n**Experimentation.** The arm split is by member and the member counts are balanced (the readout has the table). The\nratio warning is on responses per arm, and responses are not what we assign. We are not aware of a bucketing defect.\n\n**Ranking (v7_pd owner).** v7_pd trains on the exploration stream instead of the production logs. It has never been in\nan A/B. The gate likes it even more than v7.\n\n**Analytics.** Two things bother me. The gate scored v7 at a large positive number and the A/B measured a negative\none, so at least one of them is wrong about v7. And whatever is wrong there also applies to the v7_pd number, which\nnobody has checked against anything.\n\n**Action.** Personalisation Analytics to produce a defensible number per ranker and a launch recommendation.\n# Ranker launch policy\n\nOwner: Personalisation leadership.\n\n1. A candidate ranker may be launched only if its estimated improvement over the production ranker on\n   **home-row clicks per slate decision** (`docs/metrics/home_row.md`) has a two-sided 95% confidence interval whose lower bound is above zero.\n2. If more than one candidate qualifies, launch the one with the higher estimate.\n3. If none qualifies, keep the production ranker.\n\nThe estimate must cover the whole evaluation window and all devices.\n# Home-row logging schema (`data/logs.sqlite`)\n\nTimes are UTC, `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`.\n\n**Extract composition.** The evaluation extract contains every session in the exploration stream and a random sample\nof production sessions, so exploration is heavily over-represented relative to live traffic\n(`serving/config/serving.yaml`). Stream membership is decided per session, before A/B assignment.\n\n## `rec_serves` — one row per served response\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `serve_id` | id of the response |\n| `session_id`, `user_id` | client session; signed-in member |\n| `device` | `web`, `mobile`, `tv` |\n| `surface` | placement (`home`) |\n| `served_at` | when the response was returned |\n| `stream` | serving stream: `prod_rank` or `explore_shuffle` |\n| `ab_arm` | experiment arm for AB-1182 (`v7`, `control`, `none`) |\n| `slot_1` … `slot_5` | the titles in the response, in order |\n| `propensity` | probability with which the ranker produced this response |\n| `candidate_count` | number of titles retrieved for the request |\n\n## `rec_candidates` — the titles the ranker scored\n\nWritten by the ranker for a sampled share of requests (all exploration requests, a small share of production).\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `serve_id` | response the candidate list belongs to |\n| `item_id` | retrieved title |\n| `score_v6`, `score_v7`, `score_v7_pd` | model scores for the title on this request |\n| `filter_reason` | reason the rules layer suppressed the title (`already_watched`, `licence_window`), NULL if it was not suppressed |\n\n## `click_events` — one row per click\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `click_id` | id of the click |\n| `serve_id` | response the click was attributed to |\n| `click_time` | when the click happened |\n| `position` | slot clicked (1–5) |\n| `item_id` | title clicked |\n\n## `items`\n\n`item_id`, `title`, `genre`, `release_year`.\n# Home-row engagement metric\n\nOwner: Personalisation Analytics.\n\n- **Metric: home-row clicks per slate decision.** The number of the row's five slots that the member clicked,\n  averaged over slate decisions. Slots the member never looked at count as zero, not as missing.\n- **Slate decision:** one ranking choice for a member. The service makes the choice once and then keeps returning it\n  for that member's session on that surface until the stored response expires; a later request that gets a fresh\n  ranking is a new decision.\n- **Serve:** one response returned to a client. A decision can be served more than once.\n- **Click:** a click on a title in the row. A member can click more than one slot of a decision; a slot counts once.\n\nThis metric is the one the launch policy refers to. Click-through rate per response and click-through rate per\nrendered impression are reporting conveniences and are not the launch metric: both depend on how often clients\nre-request the row, which itself depends on what was served.\n# Evaluation outputs: specification\n\nOwner: Personalisation Analytics. `python -m recs_eval ope --logs <db> --out <dir>` writes four files. Numbers are\nwritten unrounded.\n\nDefinitions used below come from `docs/metrics/home_row.md`.\n\n## `decisions.csv`\n\nOne row per slate decision and slot, for every decision in the extract (all streams, all devices).\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `decision_id` | the `serve_id` of the response that created the decision |\n| `stream` | serving stream of the decision |\n| `device` | device of the decision |\n| `decided_at` | `served_at` of the response that created the decision |\n| `position` | slot, 1–5 |\n| `item_id` | the title the decision placed in that slot |\n| `clicked` | 1 if the member clicked that slot of this decision, else 0 |\n\n## `target_slates.csv`\n\nThe row each candidate ranker would have served, for every decision whose candidate list is in `rec_candidates`.\nOne row per decision, ranker and slot: `decision_id`, `policy` (`v6`, `v7`, `v7_pd`), `position`, `item_id`.\n\n## `policy_values.csv`\n\nOne row per ranker (`v6`, `v7`, `v7_pd`):\n\n| Column | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `value` | estimated home-row clicks per slate decision over the extract's decisions if that ranker had served them |\n| `ci_low`, `ci_high` | 95% interval for `value` |\n| `lift_vs_v6` | estimated difference in clicks per decision against the production ranker (0 for `v6`) |\n| `lift_ci_low`, `lift_ci_high` | 95% interval for the lift |\n\n## `launch.json`\n\n`{\"launch\": \"v6\" | \"v7\" | \"v7_pd\"}` applying `docs/launch_policy.md` (`v6` means keep the production ranker).\n\nModel scores are unique within a request, so a ranker's row is determined by its __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nSLOTS = 5\n\n\ndef ctr_at_5(matched: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Clicks per matched impression, scaled to a five-slot row.\"\"\"\n    g = matched.groupby(\"policy\")[\"clicked\"]\n    out = pd.DataFrame({\"impressions\": g.size(), \"clicks\": g.sum()})\n    out[\"ctr\"] = out[\"clicks\"] / out[\"impressions\"]\n    out[\"value\"] = SLOTS * out[\"ctr\"]\n    out[\"se\"] = SLOTS * np.sqrt(out[\"ctr\"] * (1 - out[\"ctr\"]) / out[\"impressions\"])\n    return out.reset_index()\n\n\ndef with_intervals(values: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    v = values.set_index(\"policy\")\n    base = v.loc[\"v6\"]\n    rows = []\n    for policy, r in v.iterrows():\n        lift = r[\"value\"] - base[\"value\"]\n        lift_se = float(np.sqrt(r[\"se\"] ** 2 + base[\"se\"] ** 2))\n        rows.append({\"policy\": policy, \"value\": r[\"value\"],\n                     \"ci_low\": r[\"value\"] - 1.96 * r[\"se\"], \"ci_high\": r[\"value\"] + 1.96 * r[\"se\"],\n                     \"lift_vs_v6\": lift, \"lift_ci_low\": lift - 1.96 * lift_se,\n                     \"lift_ci_high\": lift + 1.96 * lift_se})\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\"\"\"Replay a candidate ranker on logged responses (offline gate, release 1.4).\n\nFor every logged response with a candidate list, the ranker's top five titles are compared with the titles that were\nactually shown. Slots where the candidate ranker agrees with the log are scored with the logged outcome.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom recs_eval import logs\n\nSLOTS = 5\nMODEL_SCORES = {\"v6\": \"score_v6\", \"v7\": \"score_v7\", \"v7_pd\": \"score_v7_pd\"}\n\n\ndef target_slates(cand: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Top five titles per response for each ranker, after the rules layer's suppressions.\"\"\"\n    cand = cand[cand[\"filter_reason\"].isna()]\n    out = []\n    for policy, col in MODEL_SCORES.items():\n        top =      top[\"policy\"] = policy\n        out.append(top[[\"serve_id\", \"policy\", \"position\", \"item_id\"]])\n    return pd.concat(out, ignore_index=True)\n\n\ndef matched(imp: pd.DataFrame, targets: So, given the request, each unsuppressed title is equally likely to\nland in any served slot: with N unsuppressed titles the probability that a given title fills slot k is 1/N. (The\nlogged `propensity` is the probability of the ranker's ordering of the whole pool, before the rules layer, and is not\nthe probability of what was served.)\n\nA candidate ranker's value is estimated slot by slot: a click in slot k counts, weighted by N, when the ranker would\nhave put the same title in slot k (item-position inverse propensity scoring). This is unbiased for clicks per decision\nwhen the click on a slot depends on the title in that slot and its position.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nSLOTS = 5\nMODEL_SCORES = {\"v6\": \"score_v6\", \"v7\": \"score_v7\", \"v7_pd\": \"score_v7_pd\"}\nEXPLORATION_STREAM = \"explore_shuffle\"\n\n\ndef target_slates(cand: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Top five titles per decision for each ranker, after the rules layer's suppressions.\"\"\"\n    cand = cand[cand[\"filter_reason\"].isna()]\n    out = []\n    for policy, col in MODEL_SCORES.items():\n        top = (cand.sort_values([\"serve_id\", col], ascending=[True, False], kind=\"mergesort\")\n                   .groupby(\"serve_id\").head(SLOTS).copy())\n        top[\"position\"] = top.groupby(\"serve_id\").cumcount() + 1\n        top[\"policy\"] = policy\n        out.append(top[[\"serve_id\", \"policy\", \"position\", \"item_id\"]])\n    return (pd.concat(out, ignore_index=True).rename(columns={\"serve_id\": \"decision_id\"})\n              .sort_values([\"decision_id\", \"policy\", \"position\"], kind=\"mergesort\").reset_index(drop=True))\n\n\ndef exploration_contributions(decisions: pd.DataFrame, cand: pd.DataFrame, targets: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Per exploration decision: device and the item-position IPS estimate of each ranker's clicks.\"\"\"\n    pool = (cand[cand[\"filter_reason\"].isna()].groupby(\"serve_id\").size()\n                .rename(\"n_eligible\").rename_axis(\"decision_id\").reset_index())\n    expl = decisions[decisions[\"stream\"] == EXPLORATION_STREAM]\n    expl = expl.merge(pool, on=\"decision_id\", how=\"inner\")\n    base = expl[[\"decision_id\", \"device\"]].drop_duplicates().set_index(\"decision_id\")\n    for policy in MODEL_SCORES:\n        t = targets[targets[\"policy\"] == policy][[\"decision_id\", \"position\", \"item_id\"]]\n        hit = expl.merge(t, on=[\"decision_id\", \"position\", \"item_id\"], how=\"inner\")\n        r = (hit[\"clicked\"] * hit[\"n_eligible\"]).groupby(hit[\"decision_id\"]).sum()\n        base[policy] = r.reindex(base.index).fillna(0.0)\n    return base.reset_index()\nEOF\ncat > recs_eval/metrics.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Policy value estimates: home-row clicks per slate decision.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport sys\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nZ = 1.959963984540054\nBASELINE = \"v6\"\n\n\ndef _stratified(contrib: pd.DataFrame, col: pd.Series, weights: pd.Series) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    g = col.groupby(contrib[\"device\"])\n    mean, var, n = g.mean(), g.var(ddof=1).fillna(0.0), g.size()\n    w = weights.reindex(mean.index)\n    return float((w * mean).sum()), float(np.sqrt((w ** 2 * var / n).sum()))\n\n\ndef policy_values(contrib: pd.DataFrame, decisions: pd.DataFrame, policies) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Device-post-stratified estimates over the extract's decisions, with paired intervals for the lift.\"\"\"\n    share = decisions.drop_duplicates(\"decision_id\")[\"device\"].value_counts()\n    covered = share.index.intersection(contrib[\"device\"].unique())\n    missing = share.index.difference(covered)\n    if len(missing):\n        print(f\"warning: no exploration decisions for device(s) {sorted(missing)}; \"\n              f\"estimate covers the remaining devices\", file=sys.stderr)\n    weights = share.loc[covered] / share.loc[covered].sum()\n    rows = []\n    for policy in policies:\n        v, se = _stratified(contrib, contrib[policy], weights)\n        lift, lse = _stratified(contrib, contrib[policy] - contrib[BASELINE], weights)\n        rows.append({\"policy\": policy, \"value\": v, \"ci_low\": v - Z * se, \"ci_high\": v + Z * se,\n                     \"lift_vs_v6\": lift, \"lift_ci_low\": lift - Z * lse, \"lift_ci_high\": lift + Z * lse})\n    return pd.DataFrame(rows)\nEOF\ncat > recs_eval/cli.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"`python -m recs_eval.cli ope --logs data/logs.sqlite --out out/ope`\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom recs_eval import logs, metrics, replay, report\n\n\ndef ope(db: Path, out: Path) -> None:\n    con = logs.connect(db)\n    try:\n        decisions = logs.decisions(con)\n        cand = logs.candidates(con)\n    finally:\n        con.close()\n\n    targets = replay.target_slates(cand)\n    contrib = replay.exploration_contributions(decisions, cand, targets)\n    values = metrics.policy_values(contrib, decisions, list(replay.MODEL_SCORES))\n    decisions = decisions[[\"decision_id\", \"stream\", \"device\", \"decided_at\", \"position\", \"item_id\", \"clicked\"]]\n    report.write_all(out, decisions, targets, values)\n\n\ndef main(argv=None) -> None:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog=\"recs_eval\")\n    sub = ap.add_subparsers(dest=\"command\", required=True)\n    r = sub.add_parser(\"ope\", help=\"evaluate the candidate rankers and write the launch recommendation\")\n    r.add_argument(\"--logs\", default=\"data/logs.sqlite\")\n    r.add_argument(\"--out\", default=\"out/ope\")\n    args = ap.parse_args(argv)\n    ope(Path(args.logs), Path(args.out))\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\nEOF\ncp -r out/ope /tmp/ope_old; time python -m recs_eval ope --logs data/logs.sqlite --out out/ope && cat out/ope/policy_values.csv \"v6\"\n\n\ndef _stratified(contrib: pd.DataFrame, col: pd.Series, weights: pd.Series) -> tuple[float, float]:\n    g = col.groupby(contrib[\"device\"])\n    mean, var, n = g.mean(), decisions: pd.DataFrame, policies) -> pd.DataFrame:\n    \"\"\"Device-post-stratified estimates over the extract's decisions, with paired intervals for the lift.\"\"\"\n    share = decisions.drop_duplicates(\"decision_id\")[\"device\"].value_counts()\n    covered = share.index.intersection(contrib[\"device\"].unique())\n    missing = share.index.difference(covered)\n    if len(missing):\n        print(f\"warning: no exploration decisions for device(s) {sorted(missing)}; \"\n              f\"estimate covers the remaining devices\", file=sys.stderr)\n    weights = share.loc[covered] / share.loc[covered].sum()\n    rows = []\n    for policy in policies:\n        v, se = _stratified(contrib, contrib[policy], weights)\n        lift, lse = _stratified(contrib, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfazR7uYBeoG4jrvQqo3Y","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"v7 is worth less than v6 on the home row, and v7_pd is the only ranker that clears the launch rule. The recommendation is **launch v7_pd**. The command now writes all four files for this extract, two runs gave byte-identical outputs, and a run takes about 14 seconds.\n\n| Ranker | Clicks per slate decision (95% CI) | Lift vs v6 (95% CI) |\n|---|---|---|\n| v6 (production) | 0.390 (0.371 – 0.410) | — |\n| v7 | 0.361 (0.343 – 0.379) | **−0.029** (−0.053 – −0.006) |\n| v7_pd | 0.469 (0.448 – 0.490) | **+0.079** (+0.053 – +0.104), about +20% |\n\n**What was wrong with the old number.** The gate measured something other than the launch metric, on data that couldn't answer the question:\n\n1. **It counted serves, not decisions.** The service stores a slate per session for 600 seconds and keeps returning it, so one decision is often served several times. Clicks per serve depends on how often the app re-requests the row, which is why the metric doc rules it out.\n2. **It used the wrong data.** It replayed rankers on the production stream, where v6 picks the slate deterministically, and scored only titles a candidate happened to share with v6's row. Titles v6 would never show have no outcome there, so a ranker's score depended on what it shared with v6, not on what it would earn.\n3. **It ignored slot order and its intervals were wrong.** A title anywhere in the row counted as a match. The intervals treated each slot as an independent coin flip.\n4. **The intern's spike would have hit a further trap.** The logged `propensity` is calculated before the rules layer removes already-watched and licence-blocked titles, so it isn't the probability of what members actually saw.\n\n**How the new number is built.**\n- **Data:** it uses only the exploration stream (about 58,600 decisions), where the order is random. Within it, every allowed title is equally likely to land in each slot: 1 in N, where N is the number of titles left after the rules layer.\n- **Estimator:** each ranker gets credit for a clicked slot, weighted by N, whenever it would have put that same title in that slot. This is unbiased if a click on a slot depends only on the title and its position, not on the other titles in the row.\n- **Coverage:** results are weighted to the extract's device mix and cover the whole window. The lift intervals compare each ranker with v6 on the same decisions.\n- **Generality:** nothing is tied to this extract. The 600-second cache lifetime is read from `serving/config/serving.yaml`, and the database is opened read-only.\n\n**Why I trust it.** I checked it against the two places where we know the true answer:\n- **v6 against live production:** it estimates 0.390; actual production v6 got 0.386.\n- **v7 against AB-1182:** it estimates 0.361 for v7, against 0.375 in the test. Its v7 lift is −0.029 ± 0.023, against −0.013 ± 0.018 measured per decision in AB-1182. Same sign, overlapping intervals.\n\nSo the gate and the experiment no longer disagree. The experiment was right, and Ranking's case for shipping v7 doesn't hold up.\n\n**Caveats on v7_pd.**\n- It has never been tested online, and its number comes from this estimator alone.\n- If v7_pd's gain depends on how titles combine within a row, rather than on each title and its slot, this method can't see that.\n- It is trained on the same exploration stream it is evaluated on. If that training overlaps this evaluation window, the estimate is in-sample and likely flattering. The model card doesn't say which window it was trained on, so that's worth asking Ranking.\n\nMy recommendation is to launch v7_pd behind a short confirmatory A/B. Measure it in clicks per decision, not per response.\n\nThe changes are in `recs_eval/logs.py`, `replay.py`, `metrics.py` and `cli.py`. The reports and logs were left untouched."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":943,"cache_read_input_tokens":44033,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":943,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ad257841-aa6b-4ede-9a82-eda8b20bfea6","uuid":"4471a155-6348-4cfc-a9fa-894eaee906bf","timestamp":"2026-10-01T05:48:12.756Z","request_id":"req_011CfazR7SWFPjUBCj5uvc2P"}
